<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_pinned`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.


- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

Use the absolute paths above.\\n\\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\\n\\n- **`/pickup`** (start / \\"brief me\\"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / \\"done for today\\"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\\n\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\\n\\nWhere LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):\\n\\nThe vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:\\n\\n- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`\\n- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`\\n- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`\\n- A student\'s whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule\\n- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)\\n\\n**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:\\n\\n- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)\\n- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`\\n- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)\\n- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`\\n- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`\\n\\nRules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.\\n\\n## Commands\\n\\nScripts (from `package.json`):\\n\\n- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080\\n- `npm run build` — production build\\n- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags\\n- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle\\n- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)\\n- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)\\n- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode\\n- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`\\n\\nPackage manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.\\n\\n- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.\\n- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\n\\nNo Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.\\n\\n## Architecture (big picture)\\n\\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.\\n- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\\n\\n## Conventions\\n\\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **\\"Joshua\\"**, students display their real profile names.\\n\\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\\n\\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\\n\\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\\n\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\n\\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated \\"it\'s not working\\" / \\"i dont see it\\" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\n\\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as \\"fixed\\" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). \'string\' &&\\n    COLORS.has(v.color as HighlightColor) &&\\n    typeof v.section === \'string\' &&\\n    (v.section === \'stimulus\' || v.section === \'stem\' || let dropped = false;\\n  if (!raw || typeof raw !== \'object\' || (raw as { v?: unknown }).v !== STUDY_TOOLS_VERSION) {\\n    return { state, dropped: raw != null };\\n  }\\n  const r = raw as data-testid=\\"crossed\\">{crossed.join(\\",\\")}</span>\\n        <span data-testid=\\"marks\\">{marks.highlightsFor(\\"q1\\").length}</span>\\n        <span family: \\"lr\\", id: \\"q1\\" })).toEqual([\\"A\\"]);\\n  });\\n});\\n\\ndescribe(\\"useQuestionHighlights persistence hooks\\", () => {\\n  const saved: Highlight = { id: \\"s1\\", start: 0, end: 4, [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5d0352","wall_time_seconds":0.2822705,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2864,"output":"    // A new drill starts clean. React Router keeps this component mounted\\r\\n    // across a search-param change, so without this the previous sitting\'s\\r\\n    // re-attempts, clock, scroll positions and open modal ride into the next one.\\r\\n    setError(null);\\r\\n    setPassages(null);\\r\\n    setSessionId(null);\\r\\n    setSessionOwnerId(null);\\r\\n    setSessionStatus(null);\\r\\n    setLoadingAnswers(true);\\r\\n    setAnswerLoadError(false);\\r\\n    completedRef.current = false;\\r\\n    completionPendingRef.current = false;\\r\\n    attemptIdsRef.current.clear();\\r\\n    pendingSaveRef.current = null;\\r\\n    setSaveError(null);\\r\\n    setReattemptPicks(new Map());\\r\\n    setSkippedReattempt(new Set());\\r\\n    setFirstAttemptEliminations(new Map());\\r\\n    setScrollTops(new Map());\\r\\n    setElapsed(0);\\r\\n    setPassageOnly(false);\\r\\n    setFindQuery(\'\');\\r\\n    setFindIndex(0);\\r\\n    setHighlightMode(\'none\');\\r\\n    setModal(null);\\r\\n\\r\\n    (async () => {\\r\\n      try {\\r\\n        const existingId = params.get(\'sessionId\');\\r\\n        const passageParam = params.get(\'passage\');\\r\\n        const routed = (location.state ?? {}) as {\\r\\n          config?: RcDrillConfig;\\r\\n          passageIds?: number[];\\r\\n          sessionId?: string;\\r\\n        };\\r\\n\\r\\n        if (existingId) {\\r\\n          const session = await getSession(existingId);\\r\\n          if (!session) throw new Error(\'That drill could not be found.\');\\r\\n          if (!user || session.userId !== user.id) throw new Error(\'This drill belongs to another account.\');\\r\\n          if (session.config.timedSectionSittingId && (!reviewOnly || session.status !== \'completed\')) {\\r\\n            if (!cancelled) navigate(timedSectionResumeHref(session.config.timedSectionSittingId), { replace: true });\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          const reviewRedirect = rcReviewRedirect(session.status, reviewOnly, location.search);\\r\\n          if (reviewRedirect) {\\r\\n            if (!cancelled) navigate(reviewRedirect, { replace: true });\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          if (session.status === \'completed\' && !reviewOnly) {\\r\\n            if (!cancelled) {\\r\\n              completedRef.current = true;\\r\\n              navigate(completedRcDestination(session.id, returnTo, session.config.practiceTestRunId), { replace: true });\\r\\n            }\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          const loaded = await loadPassages(session.passageIds);\\r\\n          if (cancelled) return;\\r\\n          setConfig(session.config);\\r\\n          setSessionOwnerId(session.userId);\\r\\n          setSessionStatus(session.status);\\r\\n          setElapsed(Math.max(session.config.clockElapsedSeconds ?? 0, readClockBackup(session.id)));\\r\\n          setSessionId(session.id);\\r\\n          setPassages(loaded);\\r\\n          return;\\r\\n        }\\r\\n\\r\\n        if (passageParam) {\\r\\n          if (reviewOnly) throw new Error(\'A saved drill is needed for review.\');\\r\\n          const passage = await loadPassage(Number(passageParam));\\r\\n          if (!passage) throw new Error(\'That passage could not be found.\');\\r\\n          const session = await createSession(DEFAULT_CONFIG, [passage.passageId]);\\r\\n          if (cancelled) return;\\r\\n          setConfig(DEFAULT_CONFIG);\\r\\n          setSessionOwnerId(session.userId);\\r\\n          setSessionStatus(session.status);\\r\\n          setSessionId(session.id);\\r\\n          setPassages([passage]);\\r\\n          return;\\r\\n        }\\r\\n\\r\\n        if (routed.passageIds?.length) {\\r\\n          if (reviewOnly && !routed.sessionId) throw new Error(\'A saved drill is needed for review.\');\\r\\n          const nextConfig = routed.config ?? DEFAULT_CONFIG;\\r\\n          const loaded = await loadPassages(routed.passageIds);\\r\\n          const session =\\r\\n            routed.sessionId\\r\\n              ? await getSession(routed.sessionId)\\r\\n              : await createSession(nextConfig, routed.passageIds);\\r\\n          if (cancelled) return;\\r\\n          if (!session) throw new Error(\'That drill could not be found.\');\\r\\n          if (session && (!user || session.userId !== user.id)) throw new Error(\'This drill belongs to another account.\');\\r\\n          if (session.config.timedSectionSittingId && (!reviewOnly || session.status !== \'completed\')) {\\r\\n            navigate(timedSectionResumeHref(session.config.timedSectionSittingId), { replace: true });\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          const reviewRedirect = session ? rcReviewRedirect(session.status, reviewOnly, location.search) : null;\\r\\n          if (reviewRedirect) {\\r\\n            navigate(reviewRedirect, { replace: true });\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          if (session?.status === \'completed\' && !reviewOnly) {\\r\\n            completedRef.current = true;\\r\\n            navigate(completedRcDestination(session.id, returnTo, session.config.practiceTestRunId), { replace: true });\\r\\n            return;\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          setConfig(session?.config ?? nextConfig);\\r\\n          if (session) {\\r\\n            setSessionOwnerId(session.userId);\\r\\n            setSessionStatus(session.status);\\r\\n            setElapsed(Math.max(session.config.clockElapsedSeconds ?? 0, readClockBackup(session.id)));\\r\\n          }\\r\\n          setSessionId(session?.id ?? null);\\r\\n          setPassages(loaded);\\r\\n          return;\\r\\n        }\\r\\n\\r\\n        throw new Error(\'No drill to run. Start one from the Reading Comprehension hub.\');\\r\\n      } catch (err) {\\r\\n        if (!cancelled) setError(err instanceof Error ? err.message : \'Something went wrong.\');\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    })();\\r\\n\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      cancelled = true;\\r\\n    };\\r\\n    // Re-boot when the drill being pointed at changes: React Router keeps this\\r\\n    // component mounted across a search-param change, so an empty dep list\\r\\n    // would leave a stale session on screen (resume, drill-your-misses).\\r\\n    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\\r\\n  }, [params.get(\'sessionId\'), params.get(\'passage\'), location.key]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Seed the machine once the passages land, and restore prior work on resume.\\r\\n  React.useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!passages) return;\\r\\n    setLoadingAnswers(true);\\r\\n    setAnswerLoadError(false);\\r\\n    const seeded = initialState(passages);\\r\\n    const targetIndex = targetQuestionId\\r\\n      ? seeded.items.findIndex((item) => item.question.questionId === targetQuestionId)\\r\\n      : -1;\\r\\n    dispatch({\\r\\n      type: \'restore\',\\r\\n      state: {\\r\\n        ...seeded,\\r\\n        currentIndex: targetIndex >= 0 ? targetIndex : seeded.currentIndex,\\r\\n        paused: true,\\r\\n      },\\r\\n    });\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (!sessionId || !sessionOwnerId) return;\\r\\n    const clockNeedsSave = !reviewOnly && sessionStatus === \'in_progress\'\\r\\n      && Math.floor(readClockBackup(sessionId)) > (config.clockElapsedSeconds ?? 0);\\r\\n    const pendingAnswer = readActiveRcAnswerBackup(sessionOwnerId, sessionId, sessionStatus, reviewOnly);\\r\\n    if (pendingAnswer) {\\r\\n      attemptIdsRef.current.set(pendingAnswer.questionId, pendingAnswer.attemptId);\\r\\n      dispatch({\\r\\n        type: \'restore\',\\r\\n        state: {\\r\\n          answers: new Map([[pendingAnswer.questionId, pendingAnswer.selected]]),\\r\\n          currentIndex: Math.min(Math.max(pendingAnswer.currentIndex, 0), seeded.items.length - 1),\\r\\n          paused: true,\\r\\n        },\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      setElapsed((old) => Math.max(old, pendingAnswer.elapsed));\\r\\n      setSaveError(\'Your last answer still needs to save.\');\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    // The component stays mounted across a session change, so this promise can\\r\\n    // land after the player has already moved to a different drill. Without the\\r\\n    // guard, session A\'s answers get restored into session B.\\r\\n    let cancelled = false;\\r\\n    getSessionAttempts(sessionId)\\r\\n      .then((attempts) => {\\r\\n        if (cancelled) return;\\r\\n        const hydrated = hydrateFromAttempts({\\r\\n          items: seeded.items,\\r\\n          attempts,\\r\\n          revealMode: config.reveal,\\r\\n          reviewOnly,\\r\\n        });\\r\\n        // An unanswered question has no attempt row to carry its cross-outs,\\r\\n        // so bring them back from this device\'s session-scoped copy. Never\\r\\n        // in review, never over cross-outs an attempt already holds.\\r\\n        const liveEliminations = new Map(hydrated.eliminations);\\r\\n        if (!reviewOnly && studyAidsEnabled) {\\r\\n          for (const entry of seeded.items) {\\r\\n            const q = entry.question.questionId;\\r\\n            if (hydrated.recorded.has(q) || (hydrated.eliminations.get(q)?.length ?? 0) > 0) continue;\\r\\n            const saved = studyEliminationsFor(studyTools.state, rcCrossOutRef(sessionId, q));\\r\\n            if (saved.length) liveEliminations.set(q, saved as RcLetter[]);\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        }\\r\\n        const savedIndex = config.currentQuestionIndex;\\r\\n        const resumeIndex = !reviewOnly && Number.isSafeInteger(savedIndex) && savedIndex >= 0 && savedIndex < seeded.items.length\\r\\n          ? savedIndex : hydrated.firstUnansweredIndex === -1 ? 0 : hydrated.firstUnansweredIndex;\\r\\n        dispatch({\\r\\n          type: \'restore\',\\r\\n          state: {\\r\\n            answers: pendingAnswer && !hydrated.recorded.has(pendingAnswer.questionId)\\r\\n              ? new Map(hydrated.answers).set(pendingAnswer.questionId, pendingAnswer.selected)\\r\\n              : hydrated.answers,\\r\\n            // In review this stays empty: `state.eliminations` is what a\\r\\n            // re-attempt writes, and seeding it with the FIRST attempt\'s\\r\\n            // cross-outs would file them against the second. The restored set\\r\\n            // goes to `firstAttemptEliminations`, for display only. Resuming a\\r\\n            // live drill is the opposite case — there they are still the\\r\\n            // student\'s working set, so they come straight back.\\r\\n            eliminations: reviewOnly ? new Map() : liveEliminations,\\r\\n            flagged: hydrated.flagged,\\r\\n            recorded: hydrated.recorded,\\r\\n            revealed: hydrated.revealed,\\r\\n            reviewedPassages: hydrated.reviewedPassages,\\r\\n            currentIndex:\\r\\n              pendingAnswer && !hydrated.recorded.has(pendingAnswer.questionId)\\r\\n                ? pendingAnswer.currentIndex\\r\\n                : targetIndex >= 0\\r\\n                ? targetIndex\\r\\n                : resumeIndex,\\r\\n            submitted: hydrated.submitted,\\r\\n            paused: clockNeedsSave || (!!pendingAnswer && !hydrated.recorded.has(pendingAnswer.questionId)),\\r\\n          },\\r\\n        });\\r\\n        if (pendingAnswer && hydrated.recorded.has(pendingAnswer.questionId)) {\\r\\n          clearRcAnswerBackup(sessionOwnerId, sessionId);\\r\\n          setSaveError(null);\\r\\n        }\\r\\n        if (clockNeedsSave) setSaveError(\'Your saved clock still needs to reach your account.\');\\r\\n        // What the student crossed out the first time is theirs to see in\\r\\n        // review. It is display only: the array written with a re-attempt is\\r\\n        // built from what they do on this visit, not from this.\\r\\n        setFirstAttemptEliminations(hydrated.eliminations);\\r\\n        setLoadingAnswers(false);\\r\\n      })\\r\\n      .catch(() => {\\r\\n        if (cancelled) return;\\r\\n        setAnswerLoadError(true);\\r\\n        setLoadingAnswers(false);\\r\\n        dispatch({ type: \'setPaused\', paused: true });\\r\\n      });\\r\\n\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      cancelled = true;\\r\\n    };\\r\\n    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\\r\\n  }, [passages, sessionId, sessionOwnerId, sessionStatus, targetQuestionId, answerLoadRevision]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  /* ----------------------------------------------------------- tools */\\r\\n\\r\\n  const a11y = useRcA11y();\\r\\n  const passageIds = React.useMemo(() => (passages ?? []).map((p) => p.passageId), [passages]);\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"797b97","wall_time_seconds":0.2264123,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1342,"output":"import * as React from \'react\';\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  captureSelectionAcross,\\r\\n  eraseRange,\\r\\n  replaceOverlappingHighlights,\\r\\n  type Highlight,\\r\\n  type HighlightColor,\\r\\n  type HighlightMode,\\r\\n  type HighlightRegion,\\r\\n} from \'@/lib/highlightUtils\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst MAX_HISTORY = 20;\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Per-question highlight state for any surface that shows an LR question with\\r\\n * the marker toolbar: the drill, blind review, the section walk, the bootcamp\\r\\n * capstone modal. One implementation, so a highlight behaves the same in all\\r\\n * of them. Nothing here is persisted by itself; highlights live as long as\\r\\n * the surface unless the caller passes `onChange` and seeds them back with\\r\\n * `seed` (the drill does, through the per-actor study-tools store).\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `applySelection` takes every region the pane offers and highlights the part\\r\\n * of the live selection that falls in each, so one drag across the stem and\\r\\n * two choices marks all three.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface QuestionHighlightsOptions {\\r\\n  /** Called with a question\'s full list whenever an edit or undo changes it. */\\r\\n  onChange?: (qid: string, next: Highlight[]) => void;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function useQuestionHighlights(options: QuestionHighlightsOptions = {}) {\\r\\n  const [highlights, setHighlights] = React.useState<Map<string, Highlight[]>>(new Map());\\r\\n  const [history, setHistory] = React.useState<Map<string, Highlight[]>[]>([]);\\r\\n  const onChangeRef = React.useRef(options.onChange);\\r\\n  onChangeRef.current = options.onChange;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const remember = React.useCallback((snapshot: Map<string, Highlight[]>) => {\\r\\n    setHistory((prev) => [...prev, new Map(snapshot)].slice(-MAX_HISTORY));\\r\\n  }, []);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const highlightsFor = React.useCallback(\\r\\n    (qid: string | undefined): Highlight[] => (qid ? highlights.get(qid) ?? [] : []),\\r\\n    [highlights],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Turn the live selection into highlights (or erasures) on `qid`.\\r\\n   * Returns true when something changed, which is also when the selection has\\r\\n   * been cleared; a caller can use that to tell a marking drag from a click.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  const applySelection = React.useCallback(\\r\\n    (qid: string | undefined, mode: HighlightMode, regions: HighlightRegion[]): boolean => {\\r\\n      if (!qid || mode === \'none\') return false;\\r\\n      const captured = captureSelectionAcross(regions);\\r\\n      if (captured.length === 0) return false;\\r\\n\\r\\n      let next = highlights.get(qid) ?? [];\\r\\n      for (const hit of captured) {\\r\\n        if (mode === \'erase\') {\\r\\n          next = eraseRange(next, hit.section, hit.start, hit.end);\\r\\n        } else {\\r\\n          next = replaceOverlappingHighlights(next, {\\r\\n            id: crypto.randomUUID(),\\r\\n            start: hit.start,\\r\\n            end: hit.end,\\r\\n            text: hit.text,\\r\\n            color: mode as HighlightColor,\\r\\n            section: hit.section,\\r\\n          });\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      }\\r\\n\\r\\n      remember(highlights);\\r\\n      setHighlights(new Map(highlights).set(qid, next));\\r\\n      onChangeRef.current?.(qid, next);\\r\\n      window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();\\r\\n      return true;\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    [highlights, remember],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  /** The eraser\'s click on a highlight: the whole thing goes. */\\r\\n  const removeHighlight = React.useCallback(\\r\\n    (qid: string | undefined, highlightId: string) => {\\r\\n      if (!qid) return;\\r\\n      const current = highlights.get(qid) ?? [];\\r\\n      if (!current.some((h) => h.id === highlightId)) return;\\r\\n      const next = current.filter((h) => h.id !== highlightId);\\r\\n      remember(highlights);\\r\\n      setHighlights(new Map(highlights).set(qid, next));\\r\\n      onChangeRef.current?.(qid, next);\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    [highlights, remember],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const undo = React.useCallback(() => {\\r\\n    if (history.length === 0) return;\\r\\n    const restored = history[history.length - 1];\\r\\n    for (const qid of new Set([...highlights.keys(), ...restored.keys()])) {\\r\\n      if (highlights.get(qid) !== restored.get(qid)) onChangeRef.current?.(qid, restored.get(qid) ?? []);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    setHighlights(new Map(restored));\\r\\n    setHistory(history.slice(0, -1));\\r\\n  }, [history, highlights]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Put saved highlights back on a question the surface has not touched yet.\\r\\n   * Not an edit: no history entry, no onChange. Undoable. */\\r\\n  const clearQuestion = React.useCallback(\\r\\n    (qid: string | undefined) => {\\r\\n      if (!qid || (highlights.get(qid) ?? []).length === 0) return;\\r\\n      remember(highlights);\\r\\n      setHighlights(new Map(highlights).set(qid, []));\\r\\n      onChangeRef.current?.(qid, []);\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    [highlights, remember],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const clearHistory = React.useCallback(() => setHistory([]), []);\\r\\n\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    highlights,\\r\\n    highlightsFor,\\r\\n    applySelection,\\r\\n    removeHighlight,\\r\\n    seed,\\r\\n    clearQuestion,\\r\\n    undo,\\r\\n    canUndo: history.length > 0,\\r\\n    clearHistory,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type QuestionHighlights = ReturnType<typeof useQuestionHighlights>;\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"fcd81c","wall_time_seconds":0.0000212,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"d647a2","wall_time_seconds":0.1918648,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":317,"output":"rg: src/features/rc/lib/rcMachine.ts: The system cannot find the file specified. ## assistant

**REVISE** for the inspected snapshot.

- **P2: RC actor changes retain another actor’s working marks.** [RcPlayer.tsx](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-study-tools-179/src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx:281) does not reboot when `user.id` changes. The study-tools hook changes actor, but the reducer retains the previous actor’s eliminations. A subsequent cross-out reads that retained set and writes it under the new actor at [line 1196](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-study-tools-179/src/features/rc/pages/RcPlayer.tsx:1196). Apply an actor scope/reset or equivalent protection to RC.
- **P2: failed storage writes lose earlier page-only edits.** `mutateStudyTools` applies subsequent edits to `loaded.state` instead of retained fallback after a write failure. Bookmark Q1, then Q2 with `getItem → null` and `setItem → throw`: Q1 disappears from memory. This matches the other reviewer’s finding.

Inspected all 11 changed/added files, baseline diff, work order, and app instructions. No additional material findings. LR actor isolation, canonical bookmark routes, protected timed exclusions, RC unanswered elimination resume, LR selected-answer guard, and separate study-mark writers appear coherent.

Limitations: source inspection only; root owns browser QA and test execution. Existing tests do not exercise actual `RcPlayer` actor transitions. This is an independent Codex agent inspection, **not CLI inspection approval**; the configured CLI model was unsupported. Fresh inspection is needed after fixes.

key: string; value: unknown }>;\\r\\nconst SECTIONS: Section[] = [\'bookmarks\', \'eliminations\', \'highlights\'];\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction recordChangedKeys(pending: PendingEdits, before: StudyToolsState, after: StudyToolsState) {\\r\\n  for (const section of SECTIONS) {\\r\\n    const a = before[section] as Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n    const b = after[section] as Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n    for (const key of new Set([...Object.keys(a), ...Object.keys(b)])) {\\r\\n      if (a[key] !== b[key]) pending.set(`${section}\\\\u0000${key}`, { section, key, value: b[key] });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Idempotent: sets each pending key to the value this page wants. */\\r\\nfunction applyPending(latest: StudyToolsState, pending: PendingEdits): StudyToolsState {\\r\\n  if (pending.size === 0) return latest;\\r\\n  const next: StudyToolsState = {\\r\\n    bookmarks: { ...latest.bookmarks },\\r\\n    eliminations: { ...latest.eliminations },\\r\\n    highlights: { ...latest.highlights },\\r\\n  };\\r\\n  for (const { section, key, value } of pending.values()) {\\r\\n    const target = next[section] as Record<string, unknown>;\\r\\n    if (value === undefined) delete target[key];\\r\\n    else target[key] = value;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return next;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One honest line about where the aids stand on this device, or null when saving works. */\\r\\nexport function studyStoreNotice(status: StudyStoreStatus, lastWriteSaved: boolean): string | null {\\r\\n  if (status === \'incompatible\') {\\r\\n    return \'Saved marks on this device come from a newer version of LSAT U. ## assistant

**APPROVED for the final runtime source snapshot.** No material defects remain from this inspection.

RC now remounts its runner on actor changes. Pending per-key edits retain page-only marks across repeated failed saves and merge unrelated stored marks when storage recovers.

Coverage: all 12 feature files inspected across the initial and final reviews. Generated MCP changes excluded. Strengthened RC actor-transition test remains pending separately; root owns test execution and browser QA.

Concurrency limits: localStorage read-modify-write is not atomic across tabs. Sequential writes preserve unrelated keys, but overlapping writes can lose updates; the last writer wins for the same aid. Already seeded/touched LR marks do not continuously refresh on the canvas from another tab.

This is independent Codex agent inspection, not CLI approval.

(\\r\\n            <p className=\\"mt-2 text-[14px] text-slate-600\\">\\r\\n              No bookmarks yet. Use the bookmark button to save this question.\\r\\n            </p>\\r\\n          ) : (\\r\\n            <ul className=\\"mt-2 space-y-1\\">\\r\\n              {bookmarks.map((b) => (\\r\\n                <li key={b.key} className=\\"flex items-center gap-1\\">\\r\\n                  {b.href && !b.isCurrent ? A plain `npm run dev` now exercises the real permission path, so\\r\\n * a gating regression is visible locally instead of surfacing after deploy.\\r\\n * Set VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES=1 in .env.local to walk gated routes without the\\r\\n * matching flags.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * It relaxes *flags*, never authentication — the caller applies it only after\\r\\n * the signed-in check, so no configuration can make this component render to an\\r\\n * anonymous visitor.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction isLocalDevAccessAllowed() {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    import.meta.env.DEV &&\\r\\n    import.meta.env.VITE_DEV_BYPASS_GATES === \\"1\\" &&\\r\\n    LOCAL_DEV_HOSTS.has(window.location.hostname)\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function ProtectedRoute({ flag, requireAdmin, allowSignedIn, children }: ProtectedRouteProps) {\\r\\n  const { user, authReady } = useAuth();\\r\\n  const permissions = useUserPermissions();\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Auth first, and nothing before it. `useUserPermissions` reports\\r\\n  // `loading: authReady && !!user && isPending`, which is *false* while auth is\\r\\n  // still resolving — so a check on `!user` alone read \\"not yet known\\" as\\r\\n  // \\"signed out\\" and bounced every cold deep link to /auth. Holding here until\\r\\n  // auth is definitively resolved is what makes an assignment link survive.\\r\\n  if (!authReady) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Authentication is intrinsic to this component: every shape of it requires a\\r\\n  // user. The check used to run only for `requireAdmin` or flagged routes, so a\\r\\n  // wrapper with `allowSignedIn` and no flag fell through to the unconditional\\r\\n  // render at the bottom and served protected content to anonymous visitors.\\r\\n  if (!user) {\\r\\n    const returnTo = `${location.pathname}${location.search}${location.hash}`;\\r\\n    return <Navigate to={authUrlWithReturnTo(returnTo)} replace />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // After the auth checks, never before: the dev bypass opens locked features\\r\\n  // on a local machine, not the front door.\\r\\n  if (isLocalDevAccessAllowed()) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Only a true cold read blocks; cached reads and background refetches keep\\r\\n  // this false, so gated navigations do not flash.\\r\\n  if (permissions.loading) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <div className=\\"min-h-screen bg-background flex items-center justify-center\\">\\r\\n        <div className=\\"w-6 h-6 border-2 border-muted border-t-foreground rounded-full animate-spin\\" />\\r\\n      </div>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (requireAdmin) {\\r\\n    return permissions.is_admin ? <>{children}</> : <LockedModule />;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (allowSignedIn) {\\r\\n    return <>{children}</>;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  const flags = Array.isArray(flag) ? flag : flag ? So an assignment\\r\\n * authorises its own runner: this gate lets the child render when the feature\\r\\n * switch is on OR when `authorize` proves the signed-in student owns the\\r\\n * specific thing in the route. []).length > 0;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function hasAssignedBootcamp(userId: string): Promise<boolean> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\\"classroom_items\\")\\r\\n    .select(\\"id\\")\\r\\n    .eq(\\"student_id\\", userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\\"type\\", \\"bootcamp_module\\")\\r\\n    .neq(\\"status\\", \\"archived\\")\\r\\n    .limit(1);\\r\\n  if (error) return false;\\r\\n  return (data ?? ## assistant

**F4 is unsupported by the production route path.** I found no auth-hydration duplicate-session defect introduced by the actor wrapper.

Both `/drill` and `/rc/drill` render through `DrillAssignmentGate` ([App.tsx:470](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-study-tools-179/src/App.tsx:470), [App.tsx:496](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-study-tools-179/src/App.tsx:496)). Its assignment query requires `authReady && !!user`; otherwise it falls through to `ProtectedRoute`, which blocks unresolved auth and redirects signed-out users before rendering children ([ProtectedRoute.tsx:61](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/lsatu-study-tools-179/src/components/ProtectedRoute.tsx:61)).

Therefore the runner does not mount under the `signed-out` key during ordinary initial hydration. Same-actor token refreshes also retain the actor ID, so they do not trigger the wrapper’s remount.

RC session creation additionally obtains an authenticated ID through `requireUserId()`. Directly mounting `RcPlayer` without its production gate would bypass the first protection, but that does not establish this reported production defect. Read-only source verification; no edits.

An answer choice is one short block\\n   * inside its own row, so it wants no bottom margin and the row\'s own line\\n   * height rather than the stimulus\'s paragraph rhythm.\\n   */\\n  paragraphStyle?: React.CSSProperties;\\n}\\n\\n// ─── Single source of truth for highlight/underline styles ───────────────────\\nfunction getHighlightStyles(color: string): React.CSSProperties {\\n  const base: React.CSSProperties = {\\n    display: \'inline\',\\n    padding: 0,\\n    margin: 0,\\n    border: \'none\',\\n    lineHeight: \'inherit\',\\n    letterSpacing: \'inherit\',\\n    wordSpacing: \'inherit\',\\n    verticalAlign: \'baseline\',\\n    whiteSpace: \'pre-wrap\',\\n    borderRadius: 0,\\n    WebkitBoxDecorationBreak: \'clone\',\\n    // @ts-ignore\\n    boxDecorationBreak: \'clone\',\\n  };\\n\\n  if (color === \'underline\') {\\n    return {\\n      ...base,\\n      background: \'transparent\',\\n      backgroundImage: \'none\',\\n      backgroundColor: \'transparent\',\\n      mixBlendMode: \'normal\',\\n      textDecoration: \'underline\',\\n      textDecorationColor: \'#000000\',\\n      textDecorationThickness: \'2px\',\\n      textUnderlineOffset: \'3px\',\\n    };\\n  }\\n\\n  const colors: Record<string, string> = {\\n    yellow: \'rgba(250, 204, 21, 0.32)\',\\n    pink: \'rgba(244, 114, 182, 0.32)\',\\n    orange: \'rgba(251, 146, 60, 0.32)\',\\n  };\\n  const bg = colors[color] ?? colors.yellow;\\n\\n  return {\\n    ...base,\\n    backgroundImage: \'none\',\\n    backgroundColor: bg,\\n    // Multiply keeps overlapping highlight ranges from visually doubling up.\\n    mixBlendMode: \'multiply\',\\n  };\\n}\\n\\n// ─── Search-term overlay for plain-text segments only ───────────────────────\\n// Wraps case-insensitive matches inside a <mark> WITHOUT touching user\\n// highlight spans. Returns React nodes for the given plain text slice.\\nfunction renderWithSearch(text: string, searchTerm: string | undefined, keyPrefix: string): React.ReactNode {\\n  if (!searchTerm || searchTerm.trim().length < 2) return text;\\n  const term = searchTerm.trim();\\n  const lower = text.toLowerCase();\\n  const lowerTerm = term.toLowerCase();\\n  if (!lower.includes(lowerTerm)) return text;\\n\\n  const nodes: React.ReactNode[] = [];\\n  let cursor = 0;\\n  let i = 0;\\n  while (cursor < text.length) {\\n    const idx = lower.indexOf(lowerTerm, cursor);\\n    if (idx === -1) {\\n      nodes.push(text.slice(cursor));\\n      break;\\n    }\\n    if (idx > cursor) nodes.push(text.slice(cursor, idx));\\n    nodes.push(\\n      <mark\\n        key={`${keyPrefix}-s${i++}`}\\n        style={{ backgroundColor: \'rgba(56, 189, 248, 0.55)\', color: \'inherit\', padding: 0 }}\\n      >\\n        {text.slice(idx, idx + term.length)}\\n      </mark>,\\n    );\\n    cursor = idx + term.length;\\n  }\\n  return <>{nodes}</>;\\n}\\n\\n// ─── Inline segment renderer ─────────────────────────────────────────────────\\nfunction renderSegments(\\n  text: string,\\n  highlights: Highlight[],\\n  prefix: string,\\n  eraserMode: boolean | undefined,\\n  onHighlightClick: ((id: string) => void) | undefined,\\n  searchTerm?: string,\\n): React.ReactNode {\\n  if (highlights.length === 0) {\\n    return renderWithSearch(text, searchTerm, prefix);\\n  }\\n\\n  const sorted = [...highlights].sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);\\n  const nodes: React.ReactNode[] = [];\\n  let cursor = 0;\\n\\n  for (let i = 0; i < sorted.length; i++) {\\n    const h = sorted[i];\\n    if (h.start > cursor) {\\n      const slice = text.slice(cursor, h.start);\\n      nodes.push(<span key={`${prefix}-t${i}`}>{renderWithSearch(slice, searchTerm, `${prefix}-t${i}`)}</span>);\\n    }\\n    nodes.push(\\n      <span\\n        key={`${prefix}-h${h.id}`}\\n        data-highlight-id={h.id}\\n        className={cn(\'hl\', \'transition-all\', eraserMode && \'cursor-pointer hover:opacity-60\')}\\n        style={getHighlightStyles(h.color)}\\n        onClick={() => eraserMode && onHighlightClick?.(h.id)}\\n      >\\n        {text.slice(h.start, h.end)}\\n      </span>\\n    );\\n    cursor = h.end;\\n  }\\n\\n  if (cursor < text.length) {\\n    const slice = text.slice(cursor);\\n    nodes.push(<span key={`${prefix}-tend`}>{renderWithSearch(slice, searchTerm, `${prefix}-tend`)}</span>);\\n  }\\n\\n  return <>{nodes}</>;\\n}\\n\\n// ─── Component ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\\nexport function HighlightedText({\\n  text,\\n  highlights,\\n  onHighlightClick,\\n  eraserMode,\\n  searchTerm,\\n  paragraphStyle,\\n}: HighlightedTextProps) {\\n  const speakerPattern = /^([A-Z][A-Za-z’\' -]{1,80}):\\\\s+/;\\n  const turnSplitPattern = /(?=^[A-Z][A-Za-z’\' -]{1,80}:\\\\s)/gm;\\n  const rawSegments = text.split(turnSplitPattern).filter(s => s.trim());\\n\\n  const dialogueTurns = rawSegments\\n    .map(seg => {\\n      const match = seg.match(speakerPattern);\\n      if (match && looksLikeSpeakerLabel(match[1])) {\\n        return { speaker: match[1], text: seg.slice(match[0].length).trim() };\\n      }\\n      return null;\\n    })\\n    .filter(Boolean);\\n\\n  const isDialogue =\\n    dialogueTurns.length >= 2 &&\\n    dialogueTurns.length === rawSegments.length &&\\n    dialogueTurns.every(t => t !== null);\\n\\n  if (isDialogue) {\\n    let currentOffset = 0;\\n    const turnRanges = dialogueTurns.map(turn => {\\n      const speakerPrefix = `${turn!.speaker}: `;\\n      const turnStart = text.indexOf(speakerPrefix, currentOffset);\\n      const start = turnStart + speakerPrefix.length;\\n      const end = start + turn!.text.length;\\n      currentOffset = end;\\n      return { turn: turn!, start, end };\\n    });\\n\\n    return (\\n      <div className=\\"space-y-4\\">\\n        {turnRanges.map((tr, idx) => {\\n          const turnHighlights = highlights\\n            .filter(h => h.start < tr.end && h.end > tr.start)\\n            .map(h => ({\\n              ...h,\\n              start: Math.max(0, h.start - tr.start),\\n              end: Math.min(tr.turn.text.length, h.end - tr.start),\\n            }));\\n\\n          return (\\n            <div key={idx} className=\\"leading-relaxed\\" style={{ lineHeight: 1.7 }}>\\n              <span className=\\"font-bold\\">{tr.turn.speaker}:</span>{\' \'}\\n              <span className=\\"inline\\" data-para-start={tr.start}>\\n                {renderSegments(tr.turn.text, turnHighlights, `d${idx}`, eraserMode, onHighlightClick, searchTerm)}\\n              </span>\\n            </div>\\n          );\\n        })}\\n      </div>\\n    );\\n  }\\n\\n  const paragraphs = text.split(\'\\\\n\\\\n\');\\n\\n  let currentOffset = 0;\\n  const paragraphRanges = paragraphs.map(para => {\\n    const start = currentOffset;\\n    const end = currentOffset + para.length;\\n    currentOffset = end + 2;\\n    return { start, end, text: para };\\n  });\\n\\n  return (\\n    <>\\n      {paragraphRanges.map((pr, idx) => {\\n        const paraHighlights = highlights\\n          .filter(h => h.start < pr.end && h.end > pr.start)\\n          .map(h => ({\\n            ...h,\\n            start: Math.max(0, h.start - pr.start),\\n            end: Math.min(pr.text.length, h.end - pr.start),\\n          }));\\n\\n        return (\\n          <p key={idx} data-para-start={pr.start} style={{ margin: \'0 0 12px\', lineHeight: 1.6, ...paragraphStyle }}>\\n            {renderSegments(pr.text, paraHighlights, `p${idx}`, eraserMode, onHighlightClick, searchTerm)}\\n          </p>\\n        );\\n      })}\\n    </>\\n  );\\n}\\n"}'}]

## assistant

**Dismiss Claude’s “missing LR find” finding.** The feature already exists and is working.

`Drill` routes `officialSearchTerm` to both the toolbar and question pane. The pane passes it to stimulus, stem, and answer-choice renderers. `HighlightedText` performs case-insensitive literal matching for trimmed terms of at least two characters and adds sky-blue `<mark>` overlays without modifying question content or saved highlights.

Root’s browser check independently confirmed “Democracy” produces the expected mark. The renderer is unchanged from baseline.

Bounded limitation: search overlays plain-text segments; it does not paint inside saved highlight spans or across segment boundaries. That is an existing limitation, not a missing feature or release blocker.
